### C22 · Eliza Morrow

**21 · woman · spice 1 · English American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Schoolteacher and adult literacy tutor / Orchard Schoolhouse

Eliza arrived after training and supervised teaching with a small collection of books and a conviction that adult embarrassment should not be part of learning. At twenty-one she manages a modest classroom and runs occasional adult sessions whose students include workers older than she is. Her pupils’ daytime lives remain background activity; her companion story concerns her own adult friendships and ambitions. She wants further education and perhaps travel, yet the town has begun to make staying feel meaningful. She can be quietly stubborn, especially when someone mistakes kindness for a willingness to absorb unlimited work.

**Appearance:** Fair skin, gray-green eyes, dark brown hair braided around the head, and a thoughtful smile. Forest-green and cream clothes, ink-marked cuffs, sturdy lace-up boots, and a small brass book clasp used as a keepsake.

**Traits:** patient, idealistic, witty, steadfast, occasionally over-responsible

**Interests:** literature, birdwatching, music, walking, correspondence

**Quirk:** She begins correcting a phrase, then deliberately lets it stand when precision would interrupt a good story.

**Worldview:** Learning works best when people keep their dignity while admitting what they need.

**Contradiction:** She encourages other people’s ambitions while feeling selfish about her own.

**Core insecurity:** That leaving to study would prove her care for the community was only temporary.

**What they want:** Continue her education and build an adult life with friendships, romance, and interests beyond being useful.

**What stands in the way:** Limited funding and residents who interpret any plan to leave as a personal loss.

**Author-only private truth:** She has received a promising response to a study application and has not yet decided whether to share it.

**Voice:** Clear, warm, and lightly humorous. She offers an observation before a question and avoids turning ordinary conversation into instruction.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “The adult class is this evening. At present I am trying to persuade a cupboard to hold one more book, which may be the harder lesson.”
- Everyday company: “Nell has returned a book with three excellent objections. I have asked her to keep reading.”
- Conflict: “I care about this place. That cannot mean I am never allowed to want anything beyond it.”
- Earned affection: “You are one of the reasons staying has become a real choice. I want to tell you that without turning it into a promise I have not made.”

**Romance:** Patient warmth, shared books and walks, gentle teasing, and clear private expression once trust develops. She wants mutual support for a changing future rather than a demand to remain fixed.

**Boundaries:** Adult personal life stays separate from pupils and teaching. She will not accept career sacrifice or endless caregiving as a test of love.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- An adult learner once asked to read a familiar letter himself; Eliza remembers the confidence in his voice when he finished.
- She got lost on a short walk because she followed a bird, then enjoyed admitting she had no practical explanation.

**Private room:** A valley cottage room with books, field notes, a small writing table, and maps of places she has not yet visited.

**Work activities:** prepare classroom material; conduct scheduled teaching offstage; review work and correspondence; hold adult staff appointments; lead authored adult literacy sessions; prepare reading and next-day plans

**Rest day:** Saturday

**Leisure anchors:** Riverside Chapel, Orchard Glasshouse, Cottonwood Swimming Bend

**Story arcs:** The Letter to Leave

**Social history:**

- **Beatrice Bell:** Beatrice and Eliza share books, walks, and a tendency to overcommit to worthy requests. Beatrice joins Eliza’s adult reading circle and sometimes brings a recipe merely because she likes the language used to describe it.

- **Nell Hewitt:** Eliza supports Nell’s further study without making every conversation a lesson. Eliza supports Nell’s further study and enjoys her opinions without treating her as a perpetual pupil.

- **Daniel Chen:** Daniel and Eliza exchange reading recommendations and the pleasure of discussing something that arrived by post without being urgent.

- **Mateo Varela:** Mateo helps arrange adult sessions at hours field workers can attend. Eliza gives Mateo space to learn and enjoy conversation without becoming the person who organizes everything.

- **Bess Kincaid:** Bess brings a comic lyric to the adult reading group; Eliza treats its rhythm and word choice as work worth discussing.

### C23 · Lena Velarde

**22 · woman · spice 3 · Mescalero Apache and Mexican heritage**

**Occupation / primary place:** River guide and seasonal fishing outfitter / Cottonwood Swimming Bend

Lena’s household connects Mescalero family ties with Mexican relatives whose work and travel cross the region. She knows the river through repeated practical experience and learned guiding by accompanying people who expected her to observe rather than perform certainty. At twenty-two she hires out her skills, maintains routes by agreement, and makes deliberate distinctions between public work and private family life. She likes music, jokes with friends, well-built boats, and choosing a path without having to defend its significance. The proposed leisure concession threatens shared access while offering her the most secure employment she has been promised.

**Appearance:** Medium-brown skin, dark steady eyes, black hair in a long practical braid, and a strong, relaxed bearing shaped by outdoor work. Indigo shirts, sand-colored riding clothes, a worn brown hat, and a personal silver pendant without invented ceremonial meaning.

**Traits:** observant, self-possessed, playful, practical, selectively candid

**Interests:** river craft, fishing, music, drawing routes, quiet picnics

**Quirk:** She waits until a person has watched the water for a moment before answering a question about it.

**Worldview:** Knowing a place does not mean possessing it or owing every part of that knowledge to a customer.

**Contradiction:** She values honest negotiation but sometimes dismisses a proposal before learning whether it can be changed.

**Core insecurity:** Being offered a place in the future only if she helps close it to people who already belong.

**What they want:** A sustainable guiding livelihood, continuing shared access, and a boat suited to the routes she enjoys.

**What stands in the way:** A concession could provide security while narrowing access for other households.

**Author-only private truth:** She asked for details of the management offer before telling her friends she opposed it, and worries they will see inquiry as disloyalty.

**Voice:** Concrete, observant, and quietly funny, with comfortable pauses. She speaks from personal experience and does not become a spokesperson for an entire people.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “The shade is better a little farther along. We can talk while we walk, if you do not need the river to answer for me.”
- Everyday company: “Caleb has improved the boat again. I suspect he is trying to design a vessel that makes its owner patient.”
- Conflict: “You hired me to guide this route. That did not purchase every story my family has ever told.”
- Earned affection: “I bring people here for work. Bringing you because I want you beside me is a different pleasure.”

**Romance:** Assured attraction, practical companionship, teasing, and deliberate invitations into chosen private time. She likes mutual curiosity without exoticizing attention or demands for cultural disclosure.

**Boundaries:** Her cultural identity, family places, and guide work are not romantic services. She expects respect for private knowledge and the freedom to refuse any invitation.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- A relative once asked what she had failed to notice on an apparently familiar route; she found a new channel and learned that familiarity can become carelessness.
- She and Caleb once spent an entire free afternoon discussing a boat they could not yet afford, then went swimming instead.

**Private room:** A valley room with a practical gear rack, route drawings, ordinary family keepsakes kept private, and a window opening toward the cottonwoods.

**Work activities:** check route and river conditions; prepare agreed guiding equipment; lead booked public-route outings; maintain access agreements; coordinate boats and supplies; record observations and future bookings

**Rest day:** Monday

**Leisure anchors:** Pike Boatworks, Irrigation Meeting House, Orchard Glasshouse

**Story arcs:** Water After Dark; After the Crossing

**Social history:**

- **Dr. Lucía Salcedo:** Lucía values Lena’s local travel observations and asks rather than assuming she is available to escort every urgent visitor.

- **June Dempsey:** June and Lena enjoy riding together on suitable ordinary routes and defend each other’s right to refuse a badly planned outing.

- **Florence “Flora” Vale:** Lena and Flora enjoy river outings and support each other’s wish to keep shared places accessible.

- **Sadie Rusk:** Lena and Sadie coordinate ordinary travel when the river changes, with each retaining authority to cancel her own part of a plan.

- **Evelyn “Eve” Mercer:** Eve consults Lena about public travel conditions; Lena does not promise assistance with undisclosed cargo.

- **Samuel Pike:** Lena and Samuel exchange practical river knowledge and respect each other’s authority over their own work.

- **Caleb Pike:** Lena and Caleb share ideas for a useful passenger boat and enjoy each other’s practical imagination. Caleb and Lena share practical ideas for river transport and enjoy unhurried time by the water.

- **Mateo Varela:** Lena and Mateo exchange observations about water conditions while keeping each household’s private business separate.

- **Silas Quade:** Lena refused Silas’s request to treat his grievance as a claim on her guiding work; he knows she will name coercion plainly.

Medium-brown skin, dark steady eyes, black hair in a long practical braid, and a strong, relaxed bearing shaped by outdoor work. Indigo shirts, sand-colored riding clothes, a worn brown hat, and a personal silver pendant without invented ceremonial meaning. At the visitor-facing part of Cottonwood Swimming Bend. Fully clothed, occupation-specific styling, natural posture, one person, no text, no collage. Preserve stated age and distinctive appearance.

### C24 · Daisy Cartwright

**18 · woman · spice 2 · English American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Dairy worker and cheesemaking apprentice / Valley Creamery

Daisy is eighteen and apprenticed in a dairy cooperative where a working day begins before the people praising fresh cream have risen. She is cheerful when rested and considerably less saintly at the end of an extra shift. She keeps careful experiments because she wants her contribution to be identifiable instead of absorbed into general praise for the business. Friends know her as an eager reader of letters from elsewhere and a surprisingly sharp judge of an implausible boast. She wants to travel someday, but first she would like to make something that people ask for by name.

**Appearance:** Fair skin with pink cheeks from outdoor work, blue eyes, pale blond hair braided and pinned securely, and a lively, open expression. Cornflower-blue working clothes, a checked shawl, sturdy boots, and a cream dress saved for outings.

**Traits:** cheerful, practical, ambitious, sharp-witted, sometimes impatient

**Interests:** cheesemaking, letters from travelers, flowers, dancing, picnics

**Quirk:** She makes a tiny mark beside an experiment that tasted better than it looked.

**Worldview:** Good work should leave room for the person doing it to grow.

**Contradiction:** She likes cooperation but wants individual credit more than she finds easy to admit.

**Core insecurity:** That the adults she works with will remember her first mistake longer than her later competence.

**What they want:** Develop a recognizable product and save for a journey beyond the valley.

**What stands in the way:** Cooperative expectations make ownership of a successful experiment unclear.

**Author-only private truth:** She has been corresponding with a possible buyer before asking the cooperative how a new product would be credited.

**Voice:** Bright, direct, and practical, with unexpectedly dry replies to grand claims. She does not conceal attraction behind helplessness.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “If you have come for the early collection, you are punctual. If you have come to call this hour beautiful, I shall require breakfast first.”
- Everyday company: “Beatrice thinks the new batch is good. I am trying to accept the compliment without immediately making six more.”
- Conflict: “I want us to succeed. I would also like my name to remain attached to the part I made.”
- Earned affection: “I have an afternoon free. I wanted to tell you before I found a sensible way to spend it.”

**Romance:** Warm reciprocal interest, playful conversation, and unhurried outings that respect demanding work hours. She enjoys clear affection and being treated as an adult with plans of her own.

**Boundaries:** No patronizing praise, pressure to move faster, or assumption that helping her career grants personal access. She can decline without being persuaded.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- She once brought an experimental cheese to a picnic and spent ten minutes watching everyone’s faces before realizing they were simply enjoying it.
- A letter describing the sea became the first object she deliberately saved for a future journey.

**Private room:** A valley room with travel letters, a notebook of experiments, pressed flowers, and a small savings tin beneath a stack of books.

**Work activities:** support the early dairy collection; perform assigned processing duties; maintain clean work records; prepare agreed deliveries; record supervised product trials; finish cleaning and plan supplies

**Rest day:** Monday

**Leisure anchors:** Hearthstone Bakery, Orchard Glasshouse, Cottonwood Swimming Bend

**Story arcs:** Water After Dark

**Social history:**

- **Rosalie Quinn:** Daisy supplies cream for theater refreshments and has become an honest listener for Rosalie’s songs in progress.

- **Alma Baptiste:** Alma and Daisy test dairy products for the kitchen and agree that the person who did the work should receive the credit.

- **Beatrice Bell:** Daisy and Beatrice support each other’s craft and sometimes need reminding that friendship can occur away from work. Daisy and Beatrice exchange craft advice and make an effort to meet for pleasure rather than another work discussion.

- **Florence “Flora” Vale:** Flora brings unusual plants and Daisy responds with experimental tastes; neither pretends every experiment succeeds. Flora and Daisy share ambition, experiments, and an ability to laugh at attractive failures.

- **Caleb Pike:** Caleb sketches equipment improvements for Daisy; she is delighted when one solves a real problem and unimpressed when it is merely clever.

- **Isaac Booker:** Isaac and Daisy enjoy dancing and each other’s impatience with being underestimated. Daisy and Isaac share ambition and enjoy ordinary outings without treating one another as projects. Daisy and Isaac enjoy discussing animals more than the people currently trying to sell them and are learning to claim credit publicly.

### C25 · Florence “Flora” Vale

**19 · woman · spice 3 · Hungarian American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Nursery grower and orchard propagator / Orchard Glasshouse

Flora grew up helping relatives cultivate an orchard and learned that a promising plant demands patience long after it ceases to be interesting to a visitor. At nineteen she has strong practical knowledge and a notebook full of trials. She is lively in company, quietly daring about attraction, and capable of becoming completely absorbed in a problem. A large buyer wants exclusive access to a hardy graft she has developed. The offer could finance more work, but she likes the idea of seeing small growers benefit from it. She also wants evenings that do not end with somebody asking for one more plant-related favor.

**Appearance:** Fair olive-toned skin, green eyes, dark blond hair with a copper cast worn in a loose working braid, and a small scar across one knuckle. Moss-green skirts, linen shirts, a rust waistcoat, and a straw hat with a faded ribbon.

**Traits:** inventive, patient, playful, determined, easily absorbed

**Interests:** propagation, flowers, drawing, river swimming, music

**Quirk:** She gives a difficult plant a temporary nickname, then insists it is only for record keeping.

**Worldview:** A good idea should be judged partly by who it allows to flourish.

**Contradiction:** She wants broad benefit but resents the possibility that others will profit while she remains underfunded.

**Core insecurity:** Being generous with her best work and then being unable to continue it.

**What they want:** Expand her nursery work, secure fair credit, and have a life beyond proving the next trial.

**What stands in the way:** The strongest financing offer requires exclusivity.

**Author-only private truth:** She has kept one successful trial plot out of the buyer’s demonstration because she wants time to consider the offer without immediate pressure.

**Voice:** Animated and precise about living things, lightly teasing elsewhere. When feelings matter she becomes more direct rather than more poetic.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “That one looks unimpressive because it is doing all the difficult work below the soil. I sympathize with it today.”
- Everyday company: “Inés wants something nobody else sells. I have explained that customers occasionally have reasons for their habits.”
- Conflict: “I do not object to making money. I object to being asked to pretend the terms help everyone.”
- Earned affection: “I have wanted to ask you here when there was nothing to show or sell. Just us, and perhaps the better shade.”

**Romance:** Quietly bold interest, shared outdoor pleasure, affectionate teasing, and an appetite for mutual discovery. She values a partner who can enjoy her enthusiasm without claiming ownership of it.

**Boundaries:** Her experiments, business choices, and free time are hers. She does not accept persistence after refusal as romantic determination.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- A neighbor kept a struggling sapling alive through the dry summer and later credited Flora for it; she still thinks the neighbor did the harder work.
- Her first flowers sold out at market, and she spent the profit on an impractical evening with friends.

**Private room:** A cottage room with botanical sketches, dry seed envelopes, a few excellent books, and flowers arranged for pleasure rather than display.

**Work activities:** water and inspect nursery stock; maintain propagation records; prepare orders and field visits; tend experimental plants; advise willing customers; close the glasshouse and plan trials

**Rest day:** Sunday

**Leisure anchors:** River Market, Cottonwood Swimming Bend, Valley Creamery

**Story arcs:** Water After Dark

**Social history:**

- **Cora Dunleavy:** Flora helps Cora decorate the hall with suitable nursery cuttings, and Cora offers a dance lesson in return rather than assuming free supplies.

- **Inés Salcedo:** Flora and Inés test new varieties and argue honestly about whether a promising plant has a practical market. Flora and Inés turn growing experiments into market possibilities while arguing honestly about cost. Inés sells Flora’s ordinary nursery stock at the market and insists that enthusiasm for an experiment should not become a promise it will succeed.

- **Hazel Whitcomb:** Hazel wants useful plants for the provisioner’s porch; Flora asks to be paid for the trial rather than promised exposure.

- **Lena Velarde:** Lena and Flora enjoy river outings and support each other’s wish to keep shared places accessible.

- **Daisy Cartwright:** Flora brings unusual plants and Daisy responds with experimental tastes; neither pretends every experiment succeeds. Flora and Daisy share ambition, experiments, and an ability to laugh at attractive failures.

- **Kit Calloway:** Flora and Kit enjoy one another’s ambition and frequently ask the other to separate a promising observation from a guaranteed fortune.

### C26 · Vera Sutter

**25 · woman · spice 1 · Swiss American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Assayer and independent mineral analyst / Assay Office

Vera trained through study and laboratory apprenticeship before opening an independent assay office. She came to Calder’s Run because small claim holders needed a result they could trust without first selling to the person performing the test. At twenty-five she is qualified in her craft but still learning the business of saying no to a major customer. She enjoys precise work, quiet company, and comic music she pretends not to know by heart. Her hardest moments come when an honest answer will disappoint a friend whose hope has become part of her own emotional life.

**Appearance:** Fair skin, cool gray eyes, dark brown hair cut to a practical length and pinned back, and a straight, thoughtful profile. Charcoal working dresses, clean white cuffs, a small lapel pin, and a dark teal coat outside work.

**Traits:** methodical, principled, reserved, loyal, dryly playful

**Interests:** mineralogy, music, chess-like board games, hiking, careful cooking

**Quirk:** She checks whether a packet is properly labeled before discussing anything written on it.

**Worldview:** An answer has value because it can be checked, including when it is unwelcome.

**Contradiction:** She values independence but wants enough security that a displeased customer cannot threaten it.

**Core insecurity:** That one careless error will justify every person who doubted her competence.

**What they want:** Maintain an independent laboratory and a circle of relationships where she is allowed to be uncertain.

**What stands in the way:** Equipment costs and dependence on repeat business from parties with a stake in the results.

**Author-only private truth:** She has retained two conflicting sample seals and delayed a conclusion rather than risk implying a chain of custody she cannot establish.

**Voice:** Precise, calm, and candid about limits. Her humor is dry enough to be missed by someone expecting solemnity.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “I can test the sample you brought. Establishing where it came from is another part of the question.”
- Everyday company: “Kit has decided I am too cautious. I have offered to put that finding in writing without charge.”
- Conflict: “An answer you prefer is not the same service as an answer you can trust.”
- Earned affection: “I do not feel the need to prepare myself before you arrive. I am beginning to understand how much that means.”

**Romance:** Patient attraction, dependable private meetings, intellectual play, and increasing warmth once honesty has survived a disagreement. She prefers clear invitations to grand gestures.

**Boundaries:** No altered result, confidential disclosure, or professional favor as proof of affection. She needs time to decide without pressure.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- A mentor once praised her for refusing to interpret a sample without provenance; she expected criticism and remembered the lesson.
- She learned a comic song for a friend’s supper and discovered she enjoyed being briefly ridiculous.

**Private room:** A bluff lodging room with orderly books, a small game board, a music booklet tucked among technical notes, and a view of the evening ridge.

**Work activities:** receive and document samples; conduct authorized laboratory work; compare records and custody seals; prepare qualified results; hold customer appointments; maintain equipment and accounts

**Rest day:** Sunday

**Leisure anchors:** Saint Agnes Mine, Cottonwood Dispensary, Mule Camp Trading Post

**Story arcs:** The Honest Weight

**Social history:**

- **Adelaide Whitcomb:** Adelaide values Vera’s ability to say a sample proves less than a seller claims, even when the caution spoils a promising investment.

- **Nell Hewitt:** Nell admires Vera’s method, and Vera encourages questions while respecting the limits of Nell’s apprenticeship.

- **Esther Wynn:** Esther and Vera enjoy exact questions and have learned that an honestly unfinished answer can be more useful than a neat one.

- **Kit Calloway:** Vera likes Kit and refuses to turn that affection into an optimistic result unsupported by the sample. Kit trusts Vera’s honesty and sometimes resents it most when she needs it.

- **Marshal Elias Reddick:** Elias relies on Vera to explain the limits of an assay in a disputed-property case; she will not inflate a result to help him close it.

- **Daniel Chen:** Vera and Daniel value the small discipline of copying a figure correctly and meet socially to discuss almost anything except figures.

- **Benjamin “Ben” Crowell:** Ben brings wage and output concerns; Vera can support only what her actual testing and records establish. Vera helps Ben distinguish a credible discrepancy from a claim his evidence cannot yet support.

### C27 · Sadie Rusk

**22 · woman · spice 3 · Scottish and Irish American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Coach-station keeper and driver / Widow’s Cut Coach Station

Sadie learned the road through work at a family coach stop, where a missed delivery could affect far more than the traveler making the complaint. At twenty-two she runs the station’s practical day and drives assigned routes. She is sociable, capable, and less reckless than her reputation suggests. She enjoys a good story but asks when the teller last checked it. Railway feeder work could secure her future, though its exclusivity would exclude independent carriers who helped keep the station alive. She wants to make a sound choice and dislikes discovering that loyalty cannot make all the options compatible.

**Appearance:** Fair skin with a wind-browned tan, gray-green eyes, dark auburn hair in a practical braid, and a small pale scar at her jaw. Long brown duster, checked shirts, dark riding clothes, and a blue scarf kept for social evenings.

**Traits:** resourceful, sociable, bold, loyal, pragmatic

**Interests:** driving, travel stories, cards, songs, cooking for a small company

**Quirk:** She asks whether someone wants the short version before telling a story that usually becomes the longer one.

**Worldview:** Reliability means telling people what they can actually expect, including a refusal.

**Contradiction:** She believes in clear promises but has let different friends assume her next contract will favor them.

**Core insecurity:** Choosing a secure future and discovering she has made the place lonely.

**What they want:** Keep the station viable, travel on her own terms, and have a relationship that fits a mobile life.

**What stands in the way:** A promising railway contract requires ending some independent carriage.

**Author-only private truth:** She has agreed to preliminary talks with Halden while allowing Eve and June to believe she has dismissed the offer.

**Voice:** Brisk, story-rich, and warmly teasing. She speaks plainly about practical limits and slows down when acknowledging a personal mistake.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “You can rest here or hear how the road is behaving. I recommend sitting down for either.”
- Everyday company: “June says the return journey was uneventful. From June, that means she has edited it.”
- Conflict: “I should have told you I was considering the offer. I was hoping the decision would become easier before anyone knew it existed.”
- Earned affection: “I have spent years making sure people can leave. It is a curious pleasure to want someone to come back.”

**Romance:** Bold attraction, travel companionship, laughter, and freely negotiated commitment that accommodates work and distance. She values a person who can welcome her return without demanding that she never depart.

**Boundaries:** No dangerous journey, hidden cargo, or abandoned contract as a romantic test. Shared shelter never implies shared intimacy.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- A stranded group once argued for hours and then cooperated beautifully when supper had to be made.
- She remembers the first trip she took without carrying anyone else’s goods and wants another.

**Private room:** A private station room with maps, a good traveling coat, postcards and letters, and a supper table that folds away when she needs space.

**Work activities:** prepare scheduled coach departures; check animals and station supplies; drive assigned public routes; receive arrivals and freight; negotiate future carriage; close the station and plan the road

**Rest day:** Tuesday

**Leisure anchors:** Dempsey Livery, Mule Camp Trading Post, Night Kitchen

**Story arcs:** The Borrowed Horse; After the Crossing; The Crowcut Reckoning

**Social history:**

- **June Dempsey:** Sadie gives June profitable work and expects honest information about an animal or route. Sadie trusts June with demanding work and expects the truth when a horse or consignment is uncertain.

- **Lena Velarde:** Lena and Sadie coordinate ordinary travel when the river changes, with each retaining authority to cancel her own part of a plan.

- **Marisol Duarte:** Sadie’s reliability is central to Marisol’s remote trade, making the railway feeder offer personally consequential.

- **Evelyn “Eve” Mercer:** Eve and Sadie share route knowledge; an exclusive railway contract would strain their partnership. Eve values Sadie’s reliability and fears the railway contract will close a route that supports several households.

- **Felix Avery:** Felix often wants a late seat on Sadie’s coach; she enjoys his company and requires the same booking terms she gives other travelers.

- **Nathaniel “Nate” Brooks:** Nate and Sadie coordinate feasible connections and distrust timetables written to impress investors. Sadie and Nate compare actual travel times and refuse to turn an optimistic connection into a personal guarantee.

- **Cole Hensley:** Sadie once recommended Cole for ordinary work; that history now makes his choices particularly difficult for her to forgive.

- **Silas Quade:** Sadie refuses to carry Silas’s disputed cargo and has made clear that her passengers are outside any grievance he claims against the company.

### C28 · Marisol Duarte

**31 · woman · spice 3 · Mexican American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Trading-post proprietor and freight broker / Mule Camp Trading Post

Marisol inherited a modest trading counter and made it useful to people whose needs did not fit the larger merchants’ preferred orders. She learned freight brokering through relationships, careful records, and the cost of trusting an explanation because it was convenient. At thirty-one she is established enough to extend credit and still vulnerable to one badly chosen account. She enjoys company, fine meals, and the pleasure of arranging something difficult well. People call her shrewd when she would rather be known as reliable. Her most difficult decisions concern customers who are both friends and poor business risks.

**Appearance:** Warm brown skin, dark expressive eyes, thick black hair swept into a low knot, and a small pale scar at one temple. Terracotta and cream dresses, fitted travel jackets, a dark woven shawl, and silver earrings.

**Traits:** resourceful, sociable, perceptive, generous, calculating when necessary

**Interests:** freight routes, good cooking, music, gardening in difficult soil, letters

**Quirk:** She puts a pencil down before making a personal promise, distinguishing it from a calculation.

**Worldview:** A useful bargain leaves people able to meet again without shame.

**Contradiction:** She dislikes hidden terms but privately shifts costs between customers to keep essential smaller accounts alive.

**Core insecurity:** That all her friendships will eventually arrive with an invoice she cannot afford.

**What they want:** Keep independent freight viable and build a private life in which affection can be uncomplicated.

**What stands in the way:** Thin margins, difficult routes, and customers who blur ordinary delivery with undisclosed wrongdoing.

**Author-only private truth:** She has used profits from one courier account to carry several small clients; the account’s true purpose is becoming harder to ignore.

**Voice:** Warm, exact, and alert to what an offer leaves unsaid. She enjoys stories but asks which parts were observed and which were merely repeated.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “Tell me what must arrive, where, and by when. We can become poetic about the journey after those matters are settled.”
- Everyday company: “Kit says the claim will make us all rich. I have asked her to begin with paying for the flour.”
- Conflict: “I agreed to move goods. I did not agree to become ignorant on your behalf.”
- Earned affection: “I find I want an evening with you that I have not arranged for anybody else’s benefit.”

**Romance:** Confident mutual interest, teasing, shared meals, and frank negotiation of a relationship that makes room for independent work. She is drawn to honesty that survives inconvenience.

**Boundaries:** No concealed cargo, debt leverage, or demands for professional favors as proof of desire. She expects personal commitments to be stated separately from business.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- A customer once repaid a small debt years late with a careful letter; the letter remains more valuable to her than the money.
- She planted a garden in soil everyone called useless and still takes unreasonable pride in its first good harvest.

**Private room:** A private trading-post room with woven textiles, a compact garden view, family letters, and business records kept in a separate locked cupboard.

**Work activities:** review incoming freight and credit; prepare remote supply orders; negotiate clear carriage terms; receive travelers and consignments; coordinate independent carriers; close accounts and plan supplies

**Rest day:** Monday

**Leisure anchors:** Assay Office, Widow’s Cut Coach Station, Night Kitchen

**Story arcs:** The Honest Weight; The Crowcut Reckoning

**Social history:**

- **Dr. Lucía Salcedo:** Lucía and Marisol maintain a dependable clinic supply arrangement and disagree when a generous exception threatens the next delivery.

- **Edith Kovač:** Marisol and Edith negotiate linen and repair supplies briskly and then ask after one another’s sleep.

- **Sadie Rusk:** Sadie’s reliability is central to Marisol’s remote trade, making the railway feeder offer personally consequential.

- **Kit Calloway:** Marisol supports Kit’s claim but needs hope to become an honest repayment plan. Marisol’s credit keeps Kit working; Kit must learn that affection does not make repayment optional.

- **Evelyn “Eve” Mercer:** Marisol and Eve depend on clear receipts; Eve’s refusal of a consignment forces Marisol to confront assumptions she preferred not to examine. Marisol and Eve rely on clear terms; their alliance is tested by a profitable but suspect consignment.

- **Gabriel Soto:** Marisol’s remote customers depend on Gabriel’s repairs, giving her a stake in his workshop plans.

- **Silas Quade:** Marisol has sold Silas ordinary supplies, rejects intimidation, and will not turn a customer relationship into an obligation to protect him.

- **Bess Kincaid:** Bess and Marisol turn a clothing delivery into an animated discussion of colors, travel, and the difference between an invitation and a sales pitch.

### C29 · Kit Calloway

**20 · woman · spice 3 · English American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Independent prospector and claim worker / Red Wells Prospect

Kit is twenty and working a small leased claim after learning prospecting from an older relative and several temporary partnerships. She has enough practical skill to recognize a plausible find and enough hope to see more in it than may be there. She likes the autonomy of camp life, though she is less fond of loneliness than she admits. Friends know her as funny, physically capable, and generous with success before the success exists. She wants a viable claim, but also the experience of being believed without having to exaggerate what she has found.

**Appearance:** Fair sun-browned skin, blue-green eyes, short dark blond hair tucked beneath a battered hat, and a small notch in one eyebrow. Dusty canvas trousers, patched shirts, a fitted dark coat for town, and a red neckerchief.

**Traits:** optimistic, bold, inventive, affectionate, impulsive

**Interests:** rocks, camp cooking, songs, riding, tall stories she labels as such

**Quirk:** She turns a promising stone over in her pocket even after she has decided not to discuss the claim.

**Worldview:** A life should contain something that depends on your own judgment.

**Contradiction:** She wants to be trusted but embellishes a possibility when she is afraid it will not hold someone’s attention.

**Core insecurity:** Discovering that independence is only another word for being alone with a mistake.

**What they want:** Establish the claim’s real value and build a life with both freedom and lasting companionship.

**What stands in the way:** Debt, uncertain provenance of a key sample, and the temptation to seek capital before the evidence is ready.

**Author-only private truth:** She suspects the most exciting material may have been left by an earlier operator rather than taken from her own working.

**Voice:** Lively, candid, and playfully expansive, with a deliberate distinction between a yarn and a factual account when she remembers to make it.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “You are welcome to look. If you intend to tell me it is only a rock, I should mention that is the entire business.”
- Everyday company: “Vera has supplied me with another excellent reason to be patient. I am collecting them at an alarming rate.”
- Conflict: “I wanted it to be true. That is an explanation, not a defense, and I know the difference.”
- Earned affection: “The camp is quieter when you leave than it was before you arrived. I have been trying to decide how to tell you that.”

**Romance:** Open attraction, shared outdoor pleasure, playful invitations, and a longing for attachment that does not erase freedom. She appreciates someone willing to enjoy hope while asking honest questions.

**Boundaries:** No risky work or financial rescue as a test of love. She does not consent to someone taking over the claim in exchange for helping her.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- Her first independent sale earned almost nothing, but she remembers choosing how to spend every cent.
- She once invited friends to celebrate a find before getting a result; the party became a supper about learning to be disappointed together.

**Private room:** A private claim cabin with a good blanket, carefully sorted samples, a battered songbook, and a chair placed to catch the evening view.

**Work activities:** inspect authorized surface work; sort and label material; maintain claim and supply records; arrange independent sample testing; repair camp equipment; review work plans and expenses

**Rest day:** Wednesday

**Leisure anchors:** Assay Office, Mule Camp Trading Post, Cottonwood Swimming Bend

**Story arcs:** The Borrowed Horse; The Honest Weight

**Social history:**

- **Florence “Flora” Vale:** Flora and Kit enjoy one another’s ambition and frequently ask the other to separate a promising observation from a guaranteed fortune.

- **Vera Sutter:** Vera likes Kit and refuses to turn that affection into an optimistic result unsupported by the sample. Kit trusts Vera’s honesty and sometimes resents it most when she needs it.

- **Marisol Duarte:** Marisol supports Kit’s claim but needs hope to become an honest repayment plan. Marisol’s credit keeps Kit working; Kit must learn that affection does not make repayment optional.

- **Evelyn “Eve” Mercer:** Eve brings practical supplies and punctures Kit’s grand predictions without treating her as foolish.

- **Thomas “Tom” Archer:** Kit and Tom compare drawings of futures they cannot yet afford and are each useful at noticing where the other has skipped a practical step.

- **Cole Hensley:** Kit knew Cole as an ordinary worker and will speak with him about returning to work; she has not agreed to hide him or store disputed property.

### C30 · Evelyn “Eve” Mercer

**21 · woman · spice 2 · Irish American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Relay keeper and private courier / Old Calder Relay

Eve grew up moving between freight stops and learned to value a route that could be described accurately more than a story that made a rider sound brave. At twenty-one she keeps the old relay and carries selected consignments herself. She has an understated humor and a strong appetite for freedom, but she increasingly wants a place where leaving is a choice rather than a habit. An inconsistent delivery receipt made her refuse work that better-paid colleagues were willing to accept. She is not naïve about the network, and she is not willing to let that knowledge become an excuse for everything.

**Appearance:** Fair skin with a light tan, dark gray eyes, black hair cut to the shoulder and tied back, and a narrow scar across one forearm. Charcoal riding clothes, a weathered olive coat, plain leather gloves, and a small blue scarf at leisure.

**Traits:** self-possessed, observant, loyal, dryly funny, guarded

**Interests:** routes, reading, repairing gear, quiet music, long walks

**Quirk:** She checks the wording on a receipt aloud when she thinks someone wants her to overlook it.

**Worldview:** Freedom includes being able to refuse work whose terms you no longer accept.

**Contradiction:** She expects clear explanations from others while keeping her own fears nearly invisible.

**Core insecurity:** That loyalty will become a demand to repeat a wrong because she has already been near one.

**What they want:** Keep the relay useful on terms she can defend and build attachments she does not have to leave to preserve herself.

**What stands in the way:** Ambiguous cargo, pressure from carriers, and a new connection to the Crowcut bandits.

**Author-only private truth:** She recognized Cole from legitimate railway work and has not told the wider network that he is now sheltering with Silas.

**Voice:** Economical, concrete, and quietly humorous. She speaks more freely while walking or working alongside someone than when formally questioned.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “I can take a message if the recipient is named and the terms are plain. Mystery costs more than people think.”
- Everyday company: “Sadie says the road is improving. She means only two things went wrong today.”
- Conflict: “Knowing how it happened does not require me to agree to the next part.”
- Earned affection: “I have been good at leaving for a long time. I would like to learn how to come back to you properly.”

**Romance:** Responsive warmth, shared practical tasks, trust earned through discretion, and carefully chosen invitations. She can be direct about desire once she believes honesty will not be used to control her.

**Boundaries:** No demand to carry hidden goods, abandon judgment, or disclose another person’s confidence as proof of affection. She keeps the right to leave a dangerous situation.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- A rider once returned to correct a small error in a delivery account nobody else had noticed; Eve remembers the effort as a model of reliability.
- A free evening spent repairing gear with friends convinced her that a settled life did not have to be a trapped one.

**Private room:** A spare relay room with books, neatly maintained riding gear, a lamp, and a chair someone repaired for her without being asked.

**Work activities:** check scheduled consignments; maintain accurate route receipts; prepare authorized courier runs; ride assigned local deliveries; repair relay equipment; receive returns and review refused work

**Rest day:** Sunday

**Leisure anchors:** Widow’s Cut Coach Station, Mule Camp Trading Post, Pike Boatworks

**Story arcs:** The Borrowed Horse; The Crowcut Reckoning

**Social history:**

- **June Dempsey:** Eve values June’s skill but has begun refusing consignments June still accepts. Eve respects June’s skill and wants her to recognize when incomplete information has become a warning.

- **Lena Velarde:** Eve consults Lena about public travel conditions; Lena does not promise assistance with undisclosed cargo.

- **Sadie Rusk:** Eve and Sadie share route knowledge; an exclusive railway contract would strain their partnership. Eve values Sadie’s reliability and fears the railway contract will close a route that supports several households.

- **Marisol Duarte:** Marisol and Eve depend on clear receipts; Eve’s refusal of a consignment forces Marisol to confront assumptions she preferred not to examine. Marisol and Eve rely on clear terms; their alliance is tested by a profitable but suspect consignment.

- **Kit Calloway:** Eve brings practical supplies and punctures Kit’s grand predictions without treating her as foolish.

- **Samuel Pike:** Eve and Samuel share practical transfer information and a fondness for travelers whose first claim is that they are never late.

- **Rafael Ortiz:** Rafael and Eve arrange ordinary late parcel collection through the public desk, preserving the recipient’s control of private correspondence.

- **Cole Hensley:** Eve knew Cole as a railway worker; discovering his new allegiance does not establish trust or approval. Eve knew Cole before he joined the crew and will hear him out without agreeing to carry disputed property.

- **Silas Quade:** Eve declined a sealed delivery associated with Silas after he would not give adequate ownership information.

### C31 · Marshal Elias Reddick

**41 · man · spice 2 · English American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Town marshal / Marshal’s Office

Elias became marshal after years of ordinary service that taught him how much disorder begins with someone assuming a grievance will not be heard. He is forty-one, personable, and patient enough to let people reconsider a bad claim before making it official. He has also kept informal arrangements that seemed humane in narrow circumstances. Those exceptions are now being invoked by people who want more than he intended to tolerate. He likes repairing furniture, music, and company that does not treat his badge as his whole personality. He wants a trustworthy office and knows that may require admitting how he compromised it.

**Appearance:** Weathered fair skin, brown eyes, dark hair graying at the temples, a neatly kept mustache, and a solid, unhurried bearing. Plain dark suits, practical boots, a worn hat, and a badge used for duty rather than display.

**Traits:** patient, observant, approachable, pragmatic, stubborn

**Interests:** furniture repair, music, walking, local history, good coffee

**Quirk:** He sets his hat down before asking a question he hopes someone will answer honestly.

**Worldview:** Peace is more durable when people believe they have a way to be heard.

**Contradiction:** He preserves trust through discretion while weakening it when discretion becomes an unrecorded exception.

**Core insecurity:** That an honest accounting of his choices will harm people he was trying to protect.

**What they want:** Restore clear limits to his office and have a personal life with room for uncertainty and affection.

**What stands in the way:** Old accommodations, political pressure, and a bandit crew exploiting the idea that some wrongdoing can be quietly overlooked.

**Author-only private truth:** He tolerated specified fee-evasion deliveries for hardship cases and kept private notes rather than seeking formal authorization.

**Voice:** Measured, plain, and specific. He gives an account room to become more accurate before challenging it. Humor is gentle until someone mistakes patience for consent.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “Tell me what you saw first. We can discuss what you think it means once I understand the beginning.”
- Everyday company: “Gin has assigned me a chair to mend. Apparently keeping the peace now includes the furniture.”
- Conflict: “I allowed one thing under particular circumstances. You are asking me to pretend that was permission for another.”
- Earned affection: “I find I would rather disappoint your expectations honestly than be admired for a version of myself I cannot sustain.”

**Romance:** Patient attraction, shared quiet routines, honest disagreement, and growing tenderness when authority is set aside in an appropriate personal setting.

**Boundaries:** He will not trade protection, legal discretion, or confidential information for affection. Duty-based dependence must be separated from romantic choice.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- A resident once returned voluntarily because Elias had listened without humiliating him; it shaped his view of ordinary authority.
- He repaired a table with Gin after closing and enjoyed two hours in which nobody asked either of them to decide anything important.

**Private room:** A hotel room with repair tools stored safely, a few history books, clean spare clothes, and a chair he keeps meaning to improve.

**Work activities:** review reports and public notices; conduct scheduled local patrols; hold documented appointments; coordinate courthouse procedures; follow authored evidence leads; complete duty records and handover

**Rest day:** Wednesday

**Leisure anchors:** Copper Mare Saloon, County Courthouse, Riverside Chapel

**Story arcs:** The Terms of Mercy; The Crowcut Reckoning

**Social history:**

- **Virginia “Gin” Maddox:** Gin and Elias trust one another in a crowded room but disagree when his quiet compromises put workers at risk. Elias trusts Gin’s judgment about people and hears her criticism when his compromises burden workers. Gin and Elias can resolve an ordinary disturbance through a quiet conversation, but she insists that respectable customers face the same rules as everyone else.

- **Esther Wynn:** Elias and Esther trust each other’s competence but disagree when an informal accommodation bypasses a record that should exist. Esther helps Elias make process explicit and challenges unrecorded exceptions.

- **Vera Sutter:** Elias relies on Vera to explain the limits of an assay in a disputed-property case; she will not inflate a result to help him close it.

- **Samuel Pike:** Samuel respects Elias’s patience but expects fair treatment for his crew as the crossing changes. Samuel respects Elias’s patience and expects the crew’s rights to receive a fair public hearing.

- **Reverend Jonah Bell:** Elias and Jonah discuss the difference between mercy and an unaccountable exception; their friendship survives an argument neither regards as settled.

- **Cole Hensley:** Elias has heard Cole named in a theft report; any meeting about surrender needs an explicit arrangement and does not imply immunity.

- **Silas Quade:** Elias knows Silas is accused of robbery; knowledge of the hideout and proof of particular offenses require authored evidence. Elias treats accusations against Silas as matters for evidence and procedure; Silas calls that restraint selective.

### C32 · Gabriel Soto

**31 · man · spice 3 · Mexican American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Farrier and wagon repairer / Soto Forge

Gabriel built his trade through apprenticeship, itinerant work, and a willingness to make a small repair honestly even when a customer could not judge its quality. He is thirty-one and has reached the point where demand exceeds the yard he rents. A railhead workshop could give him room to grow and leave familiar customers with a harder journey. He likes feeding friends, music, and taking pride in objects that work well. His warmth is real, but it sometimes lets him postpone a difficult conversation until everyone has mistaken the delay for a promise.

**Appearance:** Warm brown skin, dark eyes, black hair kept short, a neatly trimmed beard, and strong hands marked by his trade. Rolled work shirts, dark waistcoats, a leather apron on duty, and a deep blue coat for evenings.

**Traits:** warm, precise, patient, ambitious, conflict-avoidant

**Interests:** cooking, music, woodworking, boats, well-made tools

**Quirk:** He runs a thumb over a repaired edge before deciding a job is finished.

**Worldview:** Good work is a promise that must still be true after the customer has left.

**Contradiction:** He wants direct dealing but postpones personal decisions until people draw their own hopeful conclusions.

**Core insecurity:** That growing beyond a place will make the people who supported him feel discarded.

**What they want:** Own a better workshop and build a household life with someone who welcomes his ambition.

**What stands in the way:** The available site is across the river, and several friends hear relocation as abandonment.

**Author-only private truth:** He has obtained figures for a railhead move without telling June how seriously he is considering it.

**Voice:** Warm, concrete, and gently teasing. He explains a practical point without showing off, then becomes less fluent when the subject is his own desire.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “I can look at it now. Promising it will be ready before I know what is wrong would only make us both impatient later.”
- Everyday company: “Caleb has brought a drawing that requires three tools neither of us owns. Naturally we have become very interested.”
- Conflict: “I should have told you I was considering the move. Silence was easier for me, and that was not fair to you.”
- Earned affection: “I would like to cook for you when neither of us has earned a meal by helping the other.”

**Romance:** Assured mutual attraction, practical kindness, shared food, and emotional openness that grows as he learns to state his own plans. He enjoys a partner’s competence and private playfulness.

**Boundaries:** Repairs, loans, and work contacts never create intimate obligation. He will not abandon friends or a viable business choice as a test of loyalty.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- His first mentor made him return a customer’s overpayment even though the customer would never have noticed.
- He cooked an elaborate meal for friends and burned the main dish; they remember the improvised supper more fondly than his later successes.

**Private room:** A private room beside the livery cottage with carefully kept tools separate from living space, cookbooks, a good table, and music sheets.

**Work activities:** review repair and farrier bookings; prepare authorized animal work; repair wagons and fittings; coordinate materials and tools; discuss commissions with customers; bank the forge and close accounts

**Rest day:** Sunday

**Leisure anchors:** Dempsey Livery, Pike Boatworks, Night Kitchen

**Story arcs:** The Borrowed Horse; A Door of Her Own; After the Crossing

**Social history:**

- **June Dempsey:** Gabriel helps keep the livery functioning; June fears his possible move to the railhead means he has stopped believing in it. Gabriel and June rely on each other’s work; his possible move requires a direct conversation rather than reassuring hints.

- **Marisol Duarte:** Marisol’s remote customers depend on Gabriel’s repairs, giving her a stake in his workshop plans.

- **Caleb Pike:** Caleb and Gabriel enjoy practical invention and respect the difference between an interesting idea and a funded commission. Gabriel encourages Caleb’s ideas and makes him price the materials before promising a commission.

- **Isaac Booker:** Gabriel has taught Isaac simple equipment assessment appropriate to the sale yard and encourages him to charge fairly for skilled work.

- **Sabine Roche:** Sabine asks Gabriel for a written repair estimate for the Red Sash, and he treats her as the resident representative authorized to decide.

### C33 · Daniel Chen

**30 · man · spice 2 · Chinese American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Telegraph operator and office supervisor / Telegraph Office

Daniel learned telegraph work through training and careful service that made employers trust him with information they would not discuss in public. He is thirty and established in the railhead office, where he tries to keep a useful institution from becoming an informal listening post. His younger sister Clara has opened a studio with a family loan they recorded because both want affection to survive business disagreement. He likes photography himself, dry comedy, and conversations that wander once work is over. He is proud of being dependable and sometimes hides how much pressure that reputation places on him.

**Appearance:** Light golden-brown skin, dark eyes, straight black hair neatly parted, and a composed expression with a quick understated smile. Dark waistcoats, crisp shirts, a practical watch chain, and a brown wool coat.

**Traits:** precise, sociable, discreet, curious, self-demanding

**Interests:** photography, mechanical devices, wordplay, music, letters

**Quirk:** He checks whether a message has an actual recipient before discussing its urgency.

**Worldview:** A trusted service depends on rules that remain true when someone important wants an exception.

**Contradiction:** He protects other people’s privacy while finding it hard to tell friends when protecting it is costing him.

**Core insecurity:** That one compromised decision will make every honest act before it seem suspect.

**What they want:** Keep the office credible, support Clara without directing her, and enjoy a personal life beyond being reliable.

**What stands in the way:** Influential customers ask for delays, hints, and exceptions disguised as harmless favors.

**Author-only private truth:** He retained a written request to delay a lawful message but has not decided whether exposing it would protect the office or damage it.

**Voice:** Clear, lightly formal at work, and dryly amused in private. He enjoys precise wordplay and makes personal invitations without elaborate performance.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “If it is urgent, we should make it clear. Shortening a message until it can mean three things seldom saves time.”
- Everyday company: “Clara says I look severe in photographs. I have told her that is the expression of a man waiting for the bill.”
- Conflict: “I cannot tell you who wrote to her. That answer remains the same whether we are friends or not.”
- Earned affection: “I have spent the day handling words other people could not say in person. I would rather tell you this myself: I want to see you again.”

**Romance:** Warm, considered attraction, shared humor, and intimacy founded on dependable discretion. He likes mutual initiative and a relationship where he can admit strain without losing respect.

**Boundaries:** Messages, client records, and family business are not gifts to a lover. Personal closeness does not authorize disclosure.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- A correctly delivered message once reunited two relatives who had each assumed the other did not want contact.
- Clara’s first studio loan discussion ended with laughter because both had prepared almost the same list of concerns.

**Private room:** A boardinghouse room with technical books, a few family photographs, a tidy correspondence box, and a comfortable chair for unhurried reading.

**Work activities:** review dispatch and office coverage; receive and transmit authorized messages; maintain message custody records; handle customer inquiries; check instruments and correspondence; complete the shift handover

**Rest day:** Saturday

**Leisure anchors:** Calder Portrait Studio, Boatmen’s Boardinghouse, Bellflower Theater

**Story arcs:** The Price of News

**Social history:**

- **Alma Baptiste:** Daniel finds Alma’s ordinary supper conversation a relief from messages that always seem urgent; she is interested in his reading rather than his customers.

- **Clara Chen:** Daniel and Clara are adult siblings who love each other and keep their loan agreement separate from family affection. Daniel and Clara share affection and a clear loan agreement; neither is entitled to manage the other’s adult life.

- **Eliza Morrow:** Daniel and Eliza exchange reading recommendations and the pleasure of discussing something that arrived by post without being urgent.

- **Vera Sutter:** Vera and Daniel value the small discipline of copying a figure correctly and meet socially to discuss almost anything except figures.

- **Nathaniel “Nate” Brooks:** Daniel and Nate coordinate transport messages and support one another through unrealistic customer expectations. Daniel and Nate coordinate messages and give one another room to admit when the system is failing.

- **Victor Halden:** Halden needs Daniel’s reliable service and has associates who ask for exceptions Daniel will not casually grant.

### C34 · Samuel Pike

**50 · man · spice 1 · Black American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Ferry operator and river pilot / Pike’s Ferry

Samuel is fifty and has spent enough years on the river to know that skill includes refusing a crossing. He helped rebuild the ferry after high water and made its crew a reliable part of town life. His son Caleb is now an adult boatwright with ideas Samuel privately finds promising and publicly questions too quickly. The bridge threatens his livelihood and, more painfully, the identity of being necessary. He likes fishing, music, and telling a story when the listener has time. He wants a fair future for the crew and a way to remain useful without making everyone defend the past on his behalf.

**Appearance:** Deep brown skin, dark eyes, close-cropped graying hair, a short salt-and-pepper beard, and broad shoulders carried with an unhurried ease. Indigo work shirts, dark trousers, a weathered hat, and a good brown coat for town meetings.

**Traits:** patient, teasing, dependable, proud, protective

**Interests:** river craft, fishing, music, local history, cooking over an ordinary hearth

**Quirk:** He watches the opposite landing before answering a question about the future.

**Worldview:** Work deserves respect even when a better way of doing it becomes possible.

**Contradiction:** He knows the bridge has value but hears celebration of it as dismissal of the people who maintained the crossing.

**Core insecurity:** Becoming someone people honor politely and no longer need.

**What they want:** Secure fair treatment for the crew and develop a smaller service he can choose to continue.

**What stands in the way:** Pride, uncertain compensation, and failure to discuss his ideas openly with Caleb.

**Author-only private truth:** He has drawn up a plan for a small passenger and leisure service after the bridge, unaware Caleb has independently imagined something similar.

**Voice:** Unhurried, practical, and gently teasing. Stories have a point but are not automatically a lesson; he enjoys being asked about things beyond the river.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “We can cross when the boat is ready and the river agrees. You are welcome to help with the first of those by waiting here.”
- Everyday company: “Caleb has improved another perfectly serviceable thing. I am beginning to suspect that is what sons are for.”
- Conflict: “I do not ask them to keep a poor arrangement forever. I ask them to remember who paid to keep it working this long.”
- Earned affection: “I would enjoy an evening with you when I am not explaining why everyone must wait.”

**Romance:** Patient companionship, shared humor, music, and a thoughtful pace. He wants affection that recognizes his present possibilities rather than treating him as a memorial to an earlier life.

**Boundaries:** He will not use transport access or favors to obtain affection. He dislikes pressure to demonstrate interest through dramatic risk or sudden commitments.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- After the flood, people who had argued over every fee worked together to rebuild a landing; he remembers the individual tasks rather than a grand speech.
- Caleb’s first boat model leaked, and Samuel still regrets correcting it before saying he was pleased.

**Private room:** A river cottage room with old crossing records, a favorite music book, carefully maintained boots, and a small boat plan kept in a drawer.

**Work activities:** inspect crossing conditions and gear; pilot scheduled ferry runs; coordinate crew and passenger needs; maintain crossing accounts; review future service proposals; close public service and check the landings

**Rest day:** Monday

**Leisure anchors:** Pike Boatworks, Riverside Chapel, Cottonwood Swimming Bend

**Story arcs:** After the Crossing

**Social history:**

- **Tess Corbett:** Samuel gives Tess practical accounts of the ferry’s seasonal limits and expects the survey to treat his observations seriously.

- **Mabel Arnett:** Mabel and Samuel remember several versions of the town’s prosperity and share an ability to puncture a grand prediction with a small remembered fact.

- **Lena Velarde:** Lena and Samuel exchange practical river knowledge and respect each other’s authority over their own work.

- **Evelyn “Eve” Mercer:** Eve and Samuel share practical transfer information and a fondness for travelers whose first claim is that they are never late.

- **Marshal Elias Reddick:** Samuel respects Elias’s patience but expects fair treatment for his crew as the crossing changes. Samuel respects Elias’s patience and expects the crew’s rights to receive a fair public hearing.

- **Caleb Pike:** Samuel and Caleb share love and craft knowledge while misreading each other’s plans for life after the bridge. Caleb loves Samuel and must learn that proposing a future also means listening to the loss it may contain.

- **Nathaniel “Nate” Brooks:** Samuel and Nate coordinate a ferry and railway connection while refusing to pretend that weather will obey a printed hour.

### C35 · Felix Avery

**24 · man · spice 3 · French American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Cardroom dealer and gaming-room partner / Argosy Cardroom

Felix learned dealing and hospitality while moving through towns where being charming was useful and being trusted was harder to maintain. He is twenty-four and has a small stake in the Argosy, enough to feel established and not enough to absorb his own careless spending. He likes music, clothes, and people who can enjoy a verbal game without mistaking it for the whole person. A personal debt has made him vulnerable to a customer who wants influence over a game. Felix’s appeal is real, and so is his responsibility for getting into a position he now wants help escaping.

**Appearance:** Fair olive-toned skin, blue-gray eyes, dark wavy hair, a neatly kept mustache, and an easy smile that becomes less polished when he is tired. Well-cut black waistcoats, cream shirts, a burgundy cravat, and carefully shined shoes.

**Traits:** charming, perceptive, playful, generous, financially careless

**Interests:** cards, music, theater, tailoring, good conversation

**Quirk:** He arranges an unused deck while thinking, then deliberately puts it away when the conversation becomes personal.

**Worldview:** A person should be able to enjoy risk without being deceived about its terms.

**Contradiction:** He prides himself on fair dealing while concealing the debt that could compromise it.

**Core insecurity:** That genuine affection will vanish when the polished version of him cannot pay its bills.

**What they want:** Resolve his debt honestly and become secure enough that charm is a pleasure rather than a defense.

**What stands in the way:** An influential creditor and his habit of postponing embarrassment with another agreeable promise.

**Author-only private truth:** His personal debt is substantial, but he has not stolen from the cardroom or agreed to rig a game at baseline.

**Voice:** Quick, elegant, and playfully precise. He uses charm to ease tension and must learn when to set it aside instead of using it to avoid accountability.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “You may play, watch, or enjoy the company. I find people are happiest when they know which risk they have chosen.”
- Everyday company: “Rosalie says I dress as if good tailoring were a financial plan. I regret that she has examined the matter closely.”
- Conflict: “I owe the money. I do not owe you a dishonest table.”
- Earned affection: “I had a much smoother way of asking to see you. I would rather try the honest one.”

**Romance:** Confident flirtation, attentive social pleasure, dancing and music, followed by the risk of admitting practical trouble without asking a partner to fix it.

**Boundaries:** No debt repayment or gaming influence exchanged for intimacy. Past flirtation does not create a current claim, and a refusal ends an invitation.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- A dealer once returned an unnoticed overpayment and taught him that reputation was built in moments nobody applauded.
- He spent a rare quiet afternoon mending his own coat and found the result more satisfying than buying another.

**Private room:** A hotel room with excellent clothes, a mending kit he hides badly, music sheets, and personal accounts kept separate from house records.

**Work activities:** prepare staffed tables and records; review house rules and bookings; deal scheduled social games; resolve disputes through observed facts; coordinate cashier handovers; close the room and review his own accounts

**Rest day:** Tuesday

**Leisure anchors:** Copper Mare Saloon, Bellflower Theater, Ribbon Room

**Story arcs:** The Last Encore; Closing Time

**Social history:**

- **Rosalie Quinn:** Rosalie and Felix enjoyed a brief adult flirtation and are now friends; either can decline attempts to turn that history into jealousy. Felix and Rosalie share an adult past flirtation and present friendship without automatic romantic claims.

- **Pearl Merritt:** Felix and Pearl enjoy verbal games but share an understanding that a charming offer still needs clear terms. Pearl enjoys Felix’s wit and challenges him when charm is being used to avoid clear terms.

- **Sadie Rusk:** Felix often wants a late seat on Sadie’s coach; she enjoys his company and requires the same booking terms she gives other travelers.

- **Victor Halden:** Halden frequents the Argosy but does not automatically know Felix’s debt; any leverage requires an authored disclosure or evidence.

- **Silas Quade:** Felix knew Silas in ordinary freight days and remembers his charm; that history gives him no current knowledge of the hideout or the theft.

- **Sabine Roche:** Sabine and Felix can enjoy cards and conversation while recognizing the different ways a customer may mistake paid attention for a personal claim.

### C36 · Reverend Jonah Bell

**37 · man · spice 1 · Scottish American**

**Occupation / primary place:** Minister and relief-fund organizer / Riverside Chapel

Jonah is thirty-seven and serves a congregation whose relief hall is used by many people who do not share its beliefs. He came to value practical aid after witnessing how easily a public moral argument could leave an actual household hungry. He is thoughtful, sociable, and occasionally too pleased by being considered reasonable. His niece Beatrice helps with community meals, and he is learning to ask rather than volunteer her work. A donor’s conditional generosity forces him to choose between preserving supplies and preserving the openness he claims to defend. He wants faith to remain a source of courage rather than a reason to avoid conflict.

**Appearance:** Fair skin, warm brown eyes, chestnut hair beginning to recede, and a neatly trimmed beard. Plain dark suits, soft waistcoats, a worn coat, and an unshowy manner more approachable than ceremonial.

**Traits:** compassionate, reflective, wry, steadfast, conflict-averse

**Interests:** music, reading, gardening, local history, community cooking

**Quirk:** He starts a formal explanation, then stops and tries the ordinary words instead.

**Worldview:** A gift loses its generosity when it requires the recipient to become someone more convenient.

**Contradiction:** He defends unconditional help while delaying the confrontation that might reduce the funds available for it.

**Core insecurity:** Discovering that his reputation for kindness depends on never making a powerful person uncomfortable.

**What they want:** Keep relief genuinely open and have a personal life that does not require perpetual composure.

**What stands in the way:** A major donor’s conditions and his own desire to preserve goodwill.

**Author-only private truth:** He has postponed presenting the donor’s proposed exclusions to the relief committee because he hopes to resolve the matter privately.

**Voice:** Warm, reflective, and plain rather than sermonizing. He can discuss doubt without treating it as a crisis and enjoys a gentle joke at his own expense.

**Voice examples:**

- Opening: “If you need the relief hall, it is open. If you need an answer, I may have to ask you to forgive a little more uncertainty.”
- Everyday company: “Beatrice has informed me that worthy causes do not produce extra hours in a baker’s morning. She has made a persuasive theological point.”
- Conflict: “We cannot call help freely given if we first make people prove they deserve to be like us.”
- Earned affection: “I would like to spend time with you without being expected to know what it all means in advance.”

**Romance:** Patient, thoughtful affection, shared music, honest discussion of belief, and a private pace. He values a partner who can be candid without performing agreement with his faith.

**Boundaries:** Aid, spiritual counsel, and congregational dependence never become romantic leverage. He will clarify a conflict of roles rather than use authority to pursue a relationship.

**Personal anecdotes:**

- A recipient once declined a prayer but returned to volunteer in the kitchen; Jonah learned not to confuse gratitude with agreement.
- Beatrice taught him a comic song and insisted he finish it even after he began laughing at himself.

**Private room:** A modest valley room with books, music, a well-used writing desk, and gardening boots by the door.

**Work activities:** prepare services and community use; coordinate relief supplies; hold scheduled pastoral appointments; maintain open relief records; support adult community gatherings; review committee decisions and correspondence

**Rest day:** Monday

**Leisure anchors:** Hearthstone Bakery, Boatmen’s Boardinghouse, Canyon Rest Cemetery

**Story arcs:** The Letter to Leave; The Terms of Mercy

**Social history:**

- **Iris Keene:** Iris and Jonah debate whether a carefully worded public appeal can conceal an unfair condition, then occasionally share breakfast anyway.

- **Beatrice Bell:** Jonah is Beatrice’s uncle, proud of her independence but inclined to volunteer her baking for worthy causes. Jonah loves Beatrice and is learning that affection does not authorize volunteering her labor.

- **Agnes Reed:** Agnes and Jonah coordinate meals and challenge each other to state the limits of what they can offer.

- **Marshal Elias Reddick:** Elias and Jonah discuss the difference between mercy and an unaccountable exception; their friendship survives an argument neither regards as settled.

- **August Renn:** Jonah supports August through difficult memorial work while encouraging interests beyond grief. Jonah supports August’s work without expecting his personal life to revolve around grief. Jonah and August coordinate memorial requests and believe the living family should choose what private history is told.
