## Story arcs

Each arc has three stages and three alternative resolutions. The endings are mutually exclusive outcomes at this level, not facts that all happen. The player can pause or decline; ordinary life continues.

### The Other Bank

**Cast:** Tess Corbett, Victor Halden, Adelaide Whitcomb, Mateo Varela, Thomas “Tom” Archer, Esther Wynn, Clara Chen

**Places:** Survey Camp, Railroad Freight Office, Bridge Works, County Courthouse, Irrigation Meeting House

**Opening:** A hot wind takes a survey sheet across the yard just as two people arrive asking Tess to sign different versions of the same conclusion.

**Stakes:** The bridge could shorten journeys and secure east-bank livelihoods. Its preferred approach would also strengthen the railway’s control of access and disturb existing water use. Supporting a bridge is not a vote for every term attached to it.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **Tess’s qualified alternative survey** — Tess Corbett; Tess shares a working copy after a specific research conversation; it establishes a possible route, not an approved design.

- **Clara’s earlier survey photograph** — Clara Chen; Clara releases a print and dates it using her appointment book; the image shows a survey marker, not anyone’s intent.

- **Victor’s authorization limit** — Victor Halden; Victor discloses the limit during negotiation, or an authorized filed reply establishes it; his private desk is not a public clue source.


**Stages:**

1. **A promise on paper.** Compare what the public proposal actually promises with what Tess can establish and the workers have observed.

2. **A storm at the works.** A sudden rise interrupts the shift. An authored scene lets Tom help colleagues withdraw while Tess records the interruption; this creates a delay, not an engineering verdict.

3. **The price of the crossing.** At a properly convened hearing, choose which supported proposal to advocate and whether to expose a misleading claim. The council’s response is a saved story outcome.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Conditional Build.** Support construction with revised access terms and review milestones. The railway may accept narrower profits; approval does not instantly finish the bridge.

- **Seek Alternative.** Fund and authorize further assessment of Tess’s route. The delay costs wages and rent while leaving a less restrictive future possible.

- **Withhold Support.** Decline to support the present franchise and help affected households through the delay. Victor remains a person with arguments, rather than vanishing as a defeated villain.


**Relationship thread:** A relationship with Tess, Victor, or a worker is tested by whether disagreement can coexist with affection. No partner must adopt the player’s civic position.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

### The Borrowed Horse

**Cast:** June Dempsey, Kit Calloway, Evelyn “Eve” Mercer, Gabriel Soto, Sadie Rusk

**Places:** Dempsey Livery, Red Wells Prospect, Old Calder Relay, Soto Forge, Widow’s Cut Coach Station

**Opening:** At the livery rail, a tired traveler calls a boarded mare by a name June has never heard. The animal turns before anybody answers.

**Stakes:** June’s business rests on trust; Kit needs transport for her claim; the person asserting ownership may also be mistaken. A quick public accusation would travel farther than a later correction.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **June’s boarding note** — June Dempsey; June shares the terms with the parties to the dispute.

- **Gabriel’s dated tack repair record** — Gabriel Soto; Gabriel confirms the work and customer with permission; a repaired item can change hands.

- **Eve’s delivery receipt** — Evelyn “Eve” Mercer; Eve provides the relevant portion after the sender authorizes it or a story hearing orders it.


**Stages:**

1. **Two names.** Hear the conflicting accounts separately and keep the animal’s immediate care distinct from the ownership argument.

2. **A road interrupted.** An afternoon storm strands the parties at the coach station. The confined wait gives them time to admit what their paperwork leaves out.

3. **A name in the ledger.** Compare the supported sequence of loans and sales, then seek an agreed return, documented purchase, or formal claim.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Agreed Return.** The mare returns to the established owner and June arranges a different hire for the person who relied on the disputed loan.

- **Documented Sale.** The parties agree a new sale on explicit terms; a practical compromise does not rewrite the earlier mistake.

- **Formal Dispute.** The claim remains contested and proceeds through an authored hearing. The player can stop participating without the horse disappearing into inventory.


**Relationship thread:** Shared care, a rain-delayed conversation, and an offered ride create room for attraction without making an animal or transport a romantic reward.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

### The Last Encore

**Cast:** Rosalie Quinn, Virginia “Gin” Maddox, Cora Dunleavy, Felix Avery, Bess Kincaid

**Places:** Bellflower Theater, Copper Mare Saloon, Junebug Dance Hall, The Red Sash

**Opening:** Rosalie’s rehearsal ends with an unfamiliar man’s letter on the piano and a final note nobody seems ready to stop hearing.

**Stakes:** A touring engagement could advance Rosalie’s career and leave friends with an empty stage, changed income, and feelings they have mistaken for a claim on her future.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **Rosalie’s conditional touring offer** — Rosalie Quinn; She chooses to discuss its actual terms after the invitation becomes relevant.

- **Gin’s released booking commitments** — Virginia “Gin” Maddox; Gin shares the public commitments affected by a departure; her entire account book remains private.

- **Cora and Bess’s proposed local program** — Cora Dunleavy; A rehearsal invitation reveals an alternative evening bill, not a plan to replace Rosalie without consultation.


**Stages:**

1. **An audience of friends.** Attend the rehearsal, ask what Rosalie wants, and discover who has already begun making plans on her behalf.

2. **The wrong toast.** At a crowded supper, Felix says something familiar enough to sound like a promise. Rosalie decides whether to correct him privately or publicly.

3. **An encore freely given.** Help arrange a farewell, negotiate a shorter engagement, or build a stronger local program without sabotaging the offer.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Take The Road.** Rosalie chooses the tour. Saved correspondence and a planned return sustain her relationship; she is not silently removed from an active route.

- **Short Engagement.** She negotiates a limited engagement if the offer supports it, leaving both local and traveling work possible.

- **Stay By Choice.** She stays for a credible artistic and financial opportunity she helped design. Staying is not proof that a lover won.


**Relationship thread:** Goodbye, ambition, a former intimacy with Felix, and new attraction can coexist. The player may offer a future without making it the price of love.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

### Water After Dark

**Cast:** Mateo Varela, Adelaide Whitcomb, Inés Salcedo, Lena Velarde, Florence “Flora” Vale, Daisy Cartwright

**Places:** Irrigation Meeting House, Whitcomb Ranch, River Market, Cottonwood Swimming Bend, Orchard Glasshouse, Valley Creamery

**Opening:** At dawn, two growers find different stories in the same wet channel. One has saved a crop; another has lost the turn she was promised.

**Stakes:** The old register mixes privilege, genuine labor, and practical accommodation. Catching one diversion will not make the whole arrangement fair or keep every field alive.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **Mateo’s late emergency entry** — Mateo Varela; Mateo admits the late entry when asked about the mismatch; it does not account for all the water.

- **Adelaide’s prior household promise** — Adelaide Whitcomb; Adelaide or the affected household consents to describing the promise.

- **The growers’ shared observations** — Inés Salcedo; Inés assembles volunteered observations with dates; local gossip is not silently converted into measurements.


**Stages:**

1. **Wet earth.** Compare authorized records with firsthand accounts and recognize the households facing immediate loss.

2. **The hot afternoon.** Help organize ordinary deliveries and shade while tempers rise at the market. A dispute between Daisy and Flora reveals different dependence on the same allocation.

3. **A turn for tomorrow.** Bring a concrete temporary arrangement and a separate reform proposal to the association. Mateo must account for his own exception.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Temporary Pool.** Members voluntarily pool part of the next turns while reviewing the register. It provides relief and leaves the larger argument alive.

- **Revised Priority.** An authored association vote changes future priority and obligations, creating both gratitude and resentment.

- **Record And Step Back.** Publish only consented or public facts and leave the vote to members. The player can remain a friend without becoming the valley’s permanent mediator.


**Relationship thread:** A walk with Lena or Mateo can become an evening with no public duty attached; Adelaide’s route asks whether love can survive a loss of control.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

### A Room Kept Warm

**Cast:** Agnes Reed, Ruth Freeman, Benjamin “Ben” Crowell, Rafael Ortiz, Dr. Lucía Salcedo

**Places:** Boatmen’s Boardinghouse, Public Washhouse, Saint Agnes Mine, Calder House Hotel, Salcedo Clinic

**Opening:** Agnes sets one extra place at supper and asks the table to talk about anything except the envelope beside her plate.

**Stakes:** Late wages and an unpaid hotel contract have shifted costs onto people with the smallest reserves. A public rescue could expose residents’ hardship and let the actual debtors escape attention.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **Ruth’s unpaid service invoices** — Ruth Freeman; Ruth shares business totals and keeps individual workers’ earnings private.

- **Ben’s documented wage delays** — Benjamin “Ben” Crowell; The mutual society authorizes a relevant summary.

- **Agnes’s own reserve account** — Agnes Reed; Agnes chooses to discuss the house’s position; residents’ names are omitted unless they consent.


**Stages:**

1. **An ordinary supper.** Help with an ordinary house need and hear the problem without turning the table into a public inquiry.

2. **Who owes whom.** Trace the actual obligations and distinguish disputed amounts from established debts.

3. **Keeping the door open.** Choose a collections effort, voluntary relief arrangement, or a smaller sustainable operating plan with the people affected.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Debts Paid.** The responsible businesses settle documented obligations through an authored agreement; the house recovers without pretending it received charity.

- **Shared Reserve.** A voluntary reserve buys time with clear limits and resident control. It cannot absorb every future failure.

- **Smaller House.** Agnes reduces commitments and arranges transitions with residents. Survival need not mean sacrificing herself indefinitely.


**Relationship thread:** Care appears in a meal, an honest limit, or company after a difficult conversation. Paying someone’s bill never buys their confidence or intimacy.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

### The Honest Weight

**Cast:** Vera Sutter, Benjamin “Ben” Crowell, Kit Calloway, Isaac Booker, Marisol Duarte

**Places:** Assay Office, Saint Agnes Mine, Red Wells Prospect, Cattle Sale Yard, Mule Camp Trading Post

**Opening:** Two assay slips lie under the same lamp. The figures differ enough to change a life; the labels differ just enough to keep Vera from calling it proof.

**Stakes:** Kit’s hopes, miners’ pay, and a trading post’s credit depend on trustworthy quantities. A dramatic accusation could destroy a livelihood before anyone establishes which sample was tested.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **Vera’s compared sample labels** — Vera Sutter; Vera explains the mismatch to the relevant claimants and releases a copy with permission.

- **Ben’s repeated weighing observations** — Benjamin “Ben” Crowell; Ben provides the dated notes and the uncertainty he recorded alongside them.

- **Isaac’s public sale descriptions** — Isaac Booker; Public catalog descriptions can be compared with permitted observation; this establishes a separate pattern to investigate.


**Stages:**

1. **Glitter and figures.** Hear the hopeful story about the claim, then ask Vera what the material actually supports.

2. **A crowded sale.** At the yard, Isaac must decide whether to correct an influential seller in front of potential employers.

3. **A finding worth keeping.** Arrange a qualified recheck with documented sample handling, then decide what supported result should be made public.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Corrected Records.** A confirmed clerical or handling error is corrected with compensation where agreed; disappointment survives even when fraud does not.

- **Supported Complaint.** Evidence supports a specific complaint, leading to an authored investigation and contested consequences.

- **Uncertain Claim.** The evidence remains insufficient. Kit must decide how much time and money to risk, and the player can support a choice without guaranteeing a strike.


**Relationship thread:** Vera values patient attention; Kit resists being treated as a foolish dreamer; Ben wants a companion who can enjoy an evening beyond the grievance book.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

### The Price of News

**Cast:** Iris Keene, Esther Wynn, Daniel Chen, Clara Chen, Victor Halden

**Places:** Keene’s Print Shop, County Courthouse, Telegraph Office, Calder Portrait Studio, Railroad Freight Office

**Opening:** Just before the press starts, Iris receives a accusation neat enough to print and incomplete enough to ruin somebody unfairly.

**Stakes:** The town needs a record it can trust. Iris also needs to pay for paper, wages, and the machine that makes independence possible.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **Iris’s conditional financing offer** — Iris Keene; Iris voluntarily discusses the proposal; it is evidence of an offer, not that she accepted it.

- **Esther’s public notice chronology** — Esther Wynn; Obtain ordinary public copies and preserve their dates.

- **An authorized message-timing confirmation** — Daniel Chen; The sender permits Daniel to confirm a relevant dispatch time; message content remains separately protected.


**Stages:**

1. **Before the ink.** Help separate a checkable allegation from inference and decide whether a correction can wait until the next edition.

2. **The easy money.** A patron makes financing sound like friendship. Iris must state what access she will and will not sell.

3. **What goes to press.** Prepare a sourced account, a limited correction, or a clear notice that the inquiry is unfinished. Iris retains the editorial decision.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Publish Supported.** The paper prints substantiated findings and faces the resulting social and commercial pressure.

- **Print Limited.** A narrow correction avoids overclaiming and disappoints people who wanted a sweeping accusation.

- **Hold And Finance.** Publication waits for evidence while Iris seeks less controlling finance; the delay is visible and open to criticism.


**Relationship thread:** For Iris, Daniel, Esther, or Clara, trust includes the right to keep a confidence and the courage to correct a person they like.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

### A Door of Her Own

**Cast:** Hazel Whitcomb, Adelaide Whitcomb, Lottie Fenwick, Nathaniel “Nate” Brooks, Gabriel Soto

**Places:** Eastbank Provisioners, Whitcomb Ranch, Crown & Cotton Mercantile, Temporary Passenger Depot, Soto Forge

**Opening:** Hazel has chosen the paint for her sign before asking Adelaide to read the loan agreement that would put the building partly under her mother’s control.

**Stakes:** A young business can need help and still deserve autonomy. Adelaide’s money could make the opening possible or extend a family argument into every future decision.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **Hazel’s proposed family loan** — Hazel Whitcomb; Hazel shares the draft as a borrower; the terms remain unagreed.

- **Lottie’s catalog-based supply comparison** — Lottie Fenwick; Lottie volunteers her own research, with no access to her employer’s private accounts.

- **Nate’s released station demand summary** — Nathaniel “Nate” Brooks; The station permits a non-personal summary of demand; passenger identities are irrelevant.


**Stages:**

1. **Paint and promises.** Help Hazel describe what she wants the business to become before everyone argues over how to fund it.

2. **The family supper.** Adelaide offers protection in the language of experience. Hazel must answer the actual terms rather than only the history between them.

3. **Opening the door.** Compare a smaller independent launch, a bounded family loan, or a partnership whose rights are explicit.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Small And Owned.** Hazel opens at a smaller scale with narrower inventory and retains decision authority.

- **Bounded Loan.** Mother and daughter agree a documented loan with limited controls and an achievable repayment plan.

- **Shared Venture.** A genuine partnership opens sooner and requires continuing shared decisions; Hazel does not magically retain sole control.


**Relationship thread:** Hazel’s attraction is adult and self-directed. Adelaide’s romance route can explore a life beyond managing her daughter’s future.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

### Closing Time

**Cast:** Alma Baptiste, Virginia “Gin” Maddox, Edith Kovač, Mabel Arnett, Pearl Merritt, Felix Avery

**Places:** Night Kitchen, Copper Mare Saloon, Blue Glass Baths, Marigold House, Ribbon Room, Argosy Cardroom

**Opening:** A closing-time meal begins as a celebration and turns when Alma discovers that the proposed partnership still calls her contribution wages.

**Stakes:** The evening district sells freedom from ordinary work to customers while its workers negotiate wages, repairs, ownership, and the right to leave.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **Alma’s proposed partnership terms** — Alma Baptiste; Alma shares the draft she has been asked to accept.

- **Edith’s corrected maintenance account** — Edith Kovač; Edith admits the diversion and supplies a corrected account to the people entitled to see it.

- **Pearl’s conditional guarantee offer** — Pearl Merritt; Pearl chooses to discuss the offer after recognizing its personal expectation.


**Stages:**

1. **The last plate.** Listen to a disagreement among friends who have all worked too long to phrase it graciously.

2. **What friendship costs.** Compare the three bargains without assuming one district-wide solution fits every business.

3. **Terms in daylight.** Support specific negotiations: an ownership share, a repair repayment plan, or finance without a patron’s claim on private affection.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Fairer Terms.** The businesses accept separate revised agreements, strengthening cooperation while preserving disagreements.

- **Independent Route.** One or more workers choose a smaller independent enterprise and strain a friendship without ending it automatically.

- **Honest Limit.** A proposed venture proves unaffordable. People keep their savings and face a less glamorous but sustainable next season.


**Relationship thread:** Gin, Alma, Edith, Mabel, Pearl, and Felix each distinguish being enjoyed from being known. An unhurried meal can matter more than a grand gesture.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

### The Letter to Leave

**Cast:** Eliza Morrow, Nell Hewitt, August Renn, Reverend Jonah Bell, Dr. Lucía Salcedo

**Places:** Orchard Schoolhouse, Cottonwood Dispensary, Canyon Rest Cemetery, Riverside Chapel, Salcedo Clinic

**Opening:** Eliza receives an education prospectus on the same afternoon someone calls the schoolhouse lucky to have a woman who will surely always remain.

**Stakes:** Learning and ambition do not end when a community finds someone useful. Nell and August also need room to become more than the roles other adults currently see.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **Eliza’s study opportunity** — Eliza Morrow; Eliza chooses to show the prospectus and application requirements.

- **Nell’s proposed training reference** — Nell Hewitt; Nell asks Lucía to discuss a reference after addressing her work mistake.

- **August’s duplicate burial entry** — August Renn; Public access or authorized family permission permits comparison; a duplicate is not evidence of a hidden death.


**Stages:**

1. **Useful people.** Attend an adult evening lesson and hear how an ordinary aspiration becomes difficult to admit.

2. **A mistake named.** Nell corrects an invoice error; August brings forward the duplicate record; neither small uncertainty becomes a sensational conspiracy.

3. **A future with an address.** Help plan study, improved local training, or a delayed application with real milestones and room for personal choice.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Go And Return.** A study absence begins with a saved correspondence plan and an authored return state.

- **Learn Here.** Local mentors commit to a credible training arrangement with boundaries on free labor.

- **Prepare Next Term.** The candidate postpones with a dated preparation plan. Delay remains a choice that can later be reconsidered.


**Relationship thread:** The gentler routes offer letters, walks, shared reading, and affection expressed without haste or an expectation to give up ambition.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

### After the Crossing

**Cast:** Samuel Pike, Caleb Pike, Lena Velarde, Gabriel Soto, Sadie Rusk

**Places:** Pike’s Ferry, Pike Boatworks, Cottonwood Swimming Bend, Soto Forge, Widow’s Cut Coach Station

**Opening:** Samuel finds a drawing for a passenger boat and recognizes an idea he has been keeping in his own desk for months.

**Stakes:** The bridge may change the ferry before either Pike is ready to say what he wants afterward. Their argument about practicality hides two different fears of becoming unnecessary.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **Samuel’s private leisure-service sketch** — Samuel Pike; Samuel shares it when he chooses to discuss his own future.

- **Caleb’s incomplete boat budget** — Caleb Pike; Caleb invites a practical review and admits which estimates remain uncertain.

- **Lena’s seasonal river observations** — Lena Velarde; Lena contributes the observations she is willing to share and retains control of her own guiding work.


**Stages:**

1. **Two beginnings.** Discover the overlapping plans through separate conversations without repeating a confidence before permission.

2. **A trial afternoon.** An authorized demonstration on a suitable day lets the group test demand and companionship, not prove safety through bravado.

3. **A business and a family.** Distinguish ownership, paid work, retirement, and the wish to remain close before selecting a structure.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Shared Service.** Father and son form a defined venture with Lena’s independent guiding agreement and room for Samuel to reduce hours.

- **Separate Lives.** Caleb builds his own service while Samuel chooses a different smaller role; love survives distinct livelihoods.

- **Wait With Purpose.** They improve present work and commission better estimates before investing. The river remains a place to enjoy in the meantime.


**Relationship thread:** Samuel’s patient route concerns being wanted beyond usefulness; Caleb’s more forward courtship invites someone into a future he is learning to share.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

### The Terms of Mercy

**Cast:** Reverend Jonah Bell, Agnes Reed, Beatrice Bell, Marshal Elias Reddick, Ruth Freeman, Inés Salcedo

**Places:** Riverside Chapel, Boatmen’s Boardinghouse, Hearthstone Bakery, Marshal’s Office, Public Washhouse, River Market

**Opening:** A donor offers the relief fund enough money to end an immediate shortage, provided the chapel agrees who deserves to receive it.

**Stakes:** Help can become a way to govern someone else’s life. Refusing a condition has a cost that may fall on people who never took part in the principled argument.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **Jonah’s written donation conditions** — Reverend Jonah Bell; Jonah releases the terms with the donor’s consent or states his own decision without publishing private correspondence.

- **Agnes’s anonymized need estimate** — Agnes Reed; Residents authorize aggregate totals while keeping individual circumstances private.

- **Beatrice’s sustainable bread costs** — Beatrice Bell; Beatrice chooses to share what she can afford; her kindness is not an unlimited supply.


**Stages:**

1. **A generous offer.** Hear what the money would immediately make possible and what its conditions would exclude.

2. **The people at the table.** Invite affected adults to shape the response. Jonah must stop treating their presence as an endorsement of his preferred answer.

3. **Mercy with terms of its own.** Choose a negotiated donation, a smaller independent fund, or a mixed arrangement whose limits are openly stated.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Conditions Revised.** The donor accepts narrower terms after an authored negotiation; the fund remains accountable to recipients as well as donors.

- **Smaller And Open.** The circle refuses exclusionary conditions and supports fewer needs with independent money and clear priorities.

- **Divided Provision.** Different groups provide separate services and continue arguing about the cost of that fragmentation.


**Relationship thread:** Jonah, Agnes, Beatrice, and Ruth can share conviction and still disagree. Their romances make room for ordinary delight beside responsibility.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

### The Red Sash Lease

**Cast:** Bess Kincaid, Sabine Roche, Mabel Arnett, Pearl Merritt, Esther Wynn, Cora Dunleavy, Rosalie Quinn

**Places:** The Red Sash, Marigold House, Ribbon Room, County Courthouse, Junebug Dance Hall, Bellflower Theater

**Opening:** In the Red Sash parlor, Bess rehearses a chorus about buying the house while Sabine quietly turns over the page containing the repair estimate.

**Stakes:** The residents could own their workplace and home. The purchase could also tie personal earnings to shared debt, especially if a patron presents control as generosity. Bess’s touring offer and Sabine’s corrected figures complicate a friendship that is usually easy.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **The lease purchase clause** — Sabine Roche; Sabine shares the residents’ copy; Esther helps compare an authorized filed version.

- **Sabine’s corrected repair and reserve estimate** — Sabine Roche; Sabine discloses the correction to residents before they vote or commit money.

- **Bess’s conditional entertainment offer** — Bess Kincaid; Bess chooses to tell Sabine and relevant collaborators; the player cannot reveal it by winning affection from another resident.


**Stages:**

1. **The house in the chorus.** Enjoy a public music evening and learn the residents’ hopes through conversation, not access to their rooms or bookings.

2. **Figures and freedom.** Discuss voting rights, individual earnings, the right to leave, and the real reserve. Bess decides when to disclose her alternative.

3. **Who holds the key.** The residents make their own recorded decision after comparing a purchase, renewed tenancy, or different arrangement. The player can help without gaining ownership of anyone’s choices.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Resident Purchase.** Residents accept a viable purchase with defined shares, reserves, and exit terms. Bess may negotiate a temporary touring absence without forfeiting personhood or friendships.

- **Better Tenancy.** They renew on improved terms and retain personal savings while preparing a later purchase.

- **Different Doors.** The purchase fails or is declined; residents arrange another workplace or housing on their own terms. The story does not require anyone to leave sex work to succeed.


**Relationship thread:** Bess and Sabine have separate romance routes. Off-duty invitations, explicit attraction, affection, and disagreement establish personal intimacy independently from paid appointments.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

### The Crowcut Reckoning

**Cast:** Cole Hensley, Silas Quade, Evelyn “Eve” Mercer, Marisol Duarte, Marshal Elias Reddick, Sadie Rusk, Benjamin “Ben” Crowell, Thomas “Tom” Archer

**Places:** Crowcut Hollow, Old Calder Relay, Mule Camp Trading Post, Marshal’s Office, Widow’s Cut Coach Station, Saint Agnes Mine

**Opening:** Eve finds Cole waiting outside the relay with a paper he keeps folding smaller. He asks for a conversation before he asks for help.

**Stakes:** A bandit crew took money that included workers’ wages. Cole learned that afterward; Silas knew before. The wronged workers, carriers under suspicion, and the crew face different risks from whatever happens next.

**Evidence and disclosures:**

- **Cole’s retained payroll receipt** — Cole Hensley; Cole brings the receipt to an agreed conversation; it can travel with him and does not require the player to enter the hideout.

- **Silas’s contradictory consignment note** — Silas Quade; Silas admits its contents, voluntarily produces it, or an authored lawful evidence recovery obtains it. Ordinary exploration cannot loot private papers.

- **The mutual society’s verified loss account** — Benjamin “Ben” Crowell; Ben shares an authorized aggregate record; it distinguishes actual losses from the crew’s claims about company money.


**Stages:**

1. **A man at the relay.** Hear Cole’s account in a neutral setting, decline involvement if desired, and establish what he is prepared to do beyond expressing regret.

2. **The hollow answers.** Only an authored invitation or lawful story transition enables Crowcut access. Cole and Silas confront the difference between grievance and their chosen acts; the player can leave or decline the meeting.

3. **What comes after.** Present supported evidence, discuss return of property and surrender through Elias, or refuse to assist the crew. The people harmed retain a voice and are not required to forgive.


**Alternative resolutions:**

- **Restitution And Surrender.** An authored agreement permits a return of identified property and arranged surrender. Legal outcomes remain separate milestones; no automatic pardon follows.

- **Cole Breaks Away.** Cole gives a truthful account and leaves the crew through a saved story transition. Silas remains opposed or reconsiders later; Cole still faces consequences.

- **Expose Or Refuse.** The player reports supported facts or declines further involvement. The story records that choice without requiring vigilantism, participation in theft, or a romantic rescue.


**Relationship thread:** Either man can sustain a complicated adult relationship only in circumstances permitting a free choice. Attraction never obliges the player to hide evidence, join the crew, or excuse coercion.

**Pause or decline:** The player can decline or pause. Characters retain their lives and disagreements; no automatic romance loss or unchosen crime follows.

## Dates and quieter scenes

These blueprints can support a clearly friendly outing or a mutual romantic invitation. The final two are specifically authored for the new Red Sash companions. They do not move characters until a particular invitation is accepted and scheduled.

### A Balcony After Supper

**Place / suggested time:** Calder House Hotel / 17:00–19:00

Share a quiet hotel balcony table after a meal. Watch the last street traffic and ask what would make a place feel like home.

**Conditions:** Public dining first; a quieter table requires availability, and guest rooms need a separate invitation.

### A Small Commission

**Place / suggested time:** Crown & Cotton Mercantile / 13:00–16:00

Browse fabric or commission a useful small object together, learning how each person makes an ordinary choice.

**Conditions:** Purchases stay optional and never serve as proof of affection.

### Bread for Two

**Place / suggested time:** Hearthstone Bakery / 09:00–11:00

Take bread to a shaded seat and invent an extravagant menu neither person is obliged to provide.

**Conditions:** Beatrice joins only off duty or during a specifically covered break.

### One More Dance

**Place / suggested time:** Junebug Dance Hall / 17:00–20:00

Ask for a dance, sit one out, and let the conversation change when the music slows.

**Conditions:** Each dance and any closer contact are invited separately.

### A Song in Progress

**Place / suggested time:** Bellflower Theater / 13:00–16:00

Attend a released rehearsal and ask the performer what the song is trying to say.

**Conditions:** Backstage access comes from a specific invitation; performers may be working.

### The Last Good Plate

**Place / suggested time:** Night Kitchen / 19:00–22:00

Share supper at the Night Kitchen and tell a story that did not make the public version of the day.

**Conditions:** Arrange off-duty company; a worker’s attention during service is professional.

### The River Window

**Place / suggested time:** Boatmen’s Boardinghouse / 18:00–20:00

Take a quiet place after communal supper, with lamplight, a shared book, and the sounds of the river.

**Conditions:** Other residents retain their privacy; the common room is not a private bedroom.

### A Boat on Paper

**Place / suggested time:** Pike Boatworks / 17:00–19:00

Draw an impossible pleasure boat, then talk about one ordinary thing each person would actually want aboard.

**Conditions:** Use the safe visitor area after work and leave tools alone unless invited.

### Market and a Meal

**Place / suggested time:** River Market / 13:00–16:00

Choose a few ingredients within an agreed budget and plan a meal somebody has time to enjoy.

**Conditions:** Any later kitchen visit is a separate invitation.

### The Unhurried Arrival

**Place / suggested time:** Temporary Passenger Depot / 17:00–19:00

Watch an ordinary arrival from the public platform and trade imagined journeys with no promise to leave today.

**Conditions:** Respect staff work and public platform boundaries.

### A Portrait Chosen Together

**Place / suggested time:** Calder Portrait Studio / 13:00–16:00

Choose a pose, a prop, or whether to sit for a portrait at all. What each person wants remembered becomes the real conversation.

**Conditions:** Clara sets the session and use of the image; no photograph implies a relationship announcement.

### Eastbank Breakfast

**Place / suggested time:** Eastbank Provisioners / 08:00–09:00

Meet on the provisioner’s porch for breakfast before the day becomes crowded.

**Conditions:** Arrange a free interval and allow for the ferry crossing.

### Cottonwood Afternoon

**Place / suggested time:** Cottonwood Swimming Bend / 13:00–16:00

Share shade, a picnic, and perhaps a swim when local conditions support it. Quiet company can be the whole afternoon.

**Conditions:** Swimming is optional and condition-gated; no rescue or daring test is required.

### A Plant with a Future

**Place / suggested time:** Orchard Glasshouse / 13:00–16:00

Choose a small plant or help with an invited ordinary nursery task, then imagine where it might stand next season.

**Conditions:** Flora determines which work is suitable; the visit does not grant ownership of her research.

### A Porch Without Business

**Place / suggested time:** Whitcomb Ranch / 17:00–19:00

An invited ranch-porch evening begins with a rule that the first cup of tea comes with no public negotiation.

**Conditions:** Adelaide or the appropriate resident offers the invitation and can end the visit.

### While the Coach Waits

**Place / suggested time:** Widow’s Cut Coach Station / 17:00–19:00

Share a station meal during a planned layover, watching travelers invent explanations for being late.

**Conditions:** A real layover and return journey are arranged; the player is not stranded to create intimacy.

### No Riches Promised

**Place / suggested time:** Assay Office / 13:00–16:00

Look at permitted mineral specimens and choose one for its appearance rather than its price.

**Conditions:** Visitor specimens only; no claim, assay result, or access to the mine is included.

### Trading Post Evening

**Place / suggested time:** Mule Camp Trading Post / 17:00–19:00

An invited shared meal becomes stories, music, and a conversation about where a road might end.

**Conditions:** Work and private supply records stay outside the social invitation.

### Bess, Off Duty

**Place / suggested time:** Bellflower Theater / 13:00–16:00

Bess invites the player to an afternoon performance discussion and then a walk, where she gets to be audience and author instead of host.

**Conditions:** Requires Bess’s explicit personal invitation and a cleared shift; it is separate from any paid appointment.

### Sabine’s Own Evening

**Place / suggested time:** Blue Glass Baths / 17:00–19:00

Meet Sabine in the baths’ public sitting area for tea before an agreed supper. She brings a novel and no shared accounts.

**Conditions:** Requires an off-duty invitation. Private bathing, touch, or any later visit is a separate choice.

## Retrieval and implementation

The accompanying JSON contains the full biographies, social records, 2,058 activity-level baseline schedule rows, private homes, 185 knowledge facts, 147 dialogue opportunities, 141 location beats, events, arcs, and dates. The schedules shown above are a compact readable view; the JSON preserves activities, arrival buffers, access, and event overlay references.

### Canon and knowledge

Apply world, location, participant, disclosure, and saved-story gates before assembling prompts. Private truths, private rooms, unknown hideout routes, and unchosen endings are authoring data, not public narration.

A weekly schedule is recurring infrastructure, not a plot reset. Resolve arcs only through authored state transitions.

Crowcut’s compound secret is deliberately split: Silas initially knows he concealed the payroll’s nature before the theft. Cole initially knows his own participation and later discovery, not Silas’s prior knowledge. A location’s private author note must not be inserted into every present character’s prompt.

### Scheduling

When activated, an event reserves its stated hour plus travel from the prior effective place and onward to the next effective place. Split or replace the affected baseline time interval in runtime; do not discard it without checking conflicts. The authoring pack keeps six baseline rows per day and separate event overlays.

Priority: saved consequential story transition → accepted appointment or date with explicit access → active recurring-event reservation with its travel legs → baseline weekly schedule.

The user-local week begins with Sunday at index 0. The diegetic year stays 1888. A character’s rest day can include a chosen group commitment; service may continue through unnamed adult staff or a reduced appointment book.

### Integration status

This is a complete, validated authoring pack. It requires an adapter to the current application schema, production identity checks, and an asset-generation pass before launch. It has not been imported or deployed. No media slot is marked ready.

The original Vharadren benchmark contained 45 characters, 45 places, six districts, 1,890 schedule rows, 246 directed social connections, 123 facts, 135 dialogue opportunities, 135 location beats, 12 arcs, and 10 events. Calder’s Run matches that layered approach and expands its counts for the four requested additions. Its personalities, disputes, geography, and voices are original to this frontier setting.

## Sources and benchmark

This world is fiction. The historical references informed limited regional texture; they do not certify the fictional county, institutions, prices, business arrangements, or every career as a documented 1888 example.

- **Vharadren original world bible** — Structural benchmark: 45 characters, 30 women, 45 public places, 6 districts, 1,890 schedule rows, 12 arcs, and authored retrieval layers.

- **[Vharadren current repository generator](https://github.com/tmoney2390test/kiveli-ai/blob/main/scripts/generate-vharadren-content.mjs)** — Checked current integration expectations. Its expanded roster and world-specific age/identity assertions must not be copied into this new world.

- **[National Park Service — Chinese Labor and the Iron Road](https://www.nps.gov/gosp/learn/historyculture/chinese-labor-and-the-iron-road.htm)** — Period texture: Chinese railway labor, surrounding communities, and the exclusion and violence affecting them. Individual Calder’s Run characters are fictional.

- **[Mescalero Apache Tribe — Our Culture](https://mescaleroapachetribe.com/our-culture/)** — Community-authored reference for Mescalero identity and reservation history. No tribal ritual or private cultural knowledge has been invented as a game mechanic.

- **[National Park Service — American Latino Theme Study: Business and Commerce](https://www.nps.gov/articles/latinothemebuisness.htm)** — Regional context for Mexican and Mexican-American commercial life in the Southwest.

- **[National Park Service — Women Who Went to the Klondike](https://www.nps.gov/articles/klgo-klondike-women.htm)** — A later-period comparison for women’s mining, lodging, and restaurant enterprises; not evidence that a particular fictional 1888 career was typical.
